Neither asks whether anything shipped. So Learn sat down to grade a 2026-09-09 forecast about a change no customer could reach. **F-72 wrote the same sentence one station earlier about Build** — *"Not one of them asks whether Build's work is OVER"* — which is what makes this a class rather than a bug. **THE CLASS HAS EXACTLY THREE MEMBERS AND ALL THREE NOW HAVE A GATE.** A station qualifies when its completion is a fact about the WORLD rather than a fact about the record: **Sense** (did it search — R-36's carry), **Build** (did the change leave — F-72 with P-03's `pr_open`/`merged`), **Ship** (did it go live — this). **Decide, Plan and Design produce records, and for those the artifact IS the completion**, so there is nothing to separate and no gate to add. That is the terminating half: the sweep does not continue, it stops at three, and a fourth would need a station whose output is a claim about something outside this database. **The fix is two gates asked from both sides**, because Ship's exit can be bypassed by a rewind or a waiver and Learn grading an unshipped change is where all of those end: Ship leaves only on a production deployment or a recorded handback; Learn refuses to start unshipped. A decline becomes `waiting-on-a-person` carrying the seat's rationale **verbatim** — it already names the two calls somebody has to make, and paraphrasing would invent a second answer to a question the seat answered better. **Neither gate spends an attempt:** a correct refusal is not a failed try, and charging the stall ceiling for one would end a track for being honest. **One thing the implementation exposed:** `submitStationByHand` files a handback deployment against the TRACK and not against a changeset, so "did this ship" has two different shapes depending on who shipped it — the same fact behind P-104, now biting in a second place. |
| **F-196**| **A test built a `Request` to prove a cookie rule, and happy-dom had already dropped the cookie — the check could not see what production sees**| **FIXED 2026-09-04 by A2 (P-135, `4360353f0`).** `test/setup.ts` preloads happy-dom's globals, and happy-dom enforces the Fetch spec's forbidden-header rules: `new Request(url, {headers: {Cookie}})` silently drops the cookie and `new Response(body, {headers: {"Set-Cookie"}})` silently drops that. Under `bun run` both survive, and under workerd — the runtime that actually serves this — both survive. So a test asserting *"a signed-in reader is never served a cached page"* was handed a request carrying **no cookie at all**: it passed for the wrong reason, and **would have gone on passing if the rule it guards were deleted**. Three of A2's own safety tests failed this way and read at first as a logic bug in the predicate. **The fix is the pattern: the predicate takes a header reader (`{get(name): string \| null}`), not a `Request` or a `Response`** — `carriesASession(headers)`, `mayUseEdgeCache(method, pathname, headers)`, `mayStoreInEdgeCache(status, headers)` — so it is a pure function of the values it decides on and the test states the rule rather than letting the environment answer for it. Direct sibling of [[F-192]]: a check that cannot see what production sees is not a check. The wider rule: **when a test constructs a web platform object, confirm the harness preserves the field under test before trusting a green.** |
| **F-197**| **`ctx.waitUntil` is UNDEFINED in the nitro Cloudflare wrapper, so every cache write was cancelled mid-flight — and a silent catch meant nothing said so**| **FIXED 2026-09-04 by A2 (P-135, `4360353f0`).** Workers cancels pending I/O the moment a response is returned, so a promise not handed to `waitUntil` does not merely finish late — **it never happens.** The Worker's own `caches.default.put` was issued on every anonymous marketing request and cancelled every time, so the cache missed forever: `X-Supaprod-Cache` read MISS on read 2, 3 and 4 of a sequence, with `put` never throwing. It was invisible for two compounding reasons: the code guarded on `typeof waitUntil === "function"` and did nothing when absent, and the write carried `.catch(() => undefined)` — **a silent catch waiting for a rejection that never came, because a cancellation is not a rejection.** Proved by instrumenting the response with the three facts (`store=true storable=true waitUntil=undefined`); `caches.default` itself was probed independently in workerd and works, put and match both. **The fix: `await` the write when `waitUntil` is absent, and report a refused write through `captureError` instead of eating it.** The rule: **never assume a runtime hook exists because the platform documents it — the wrapper you are inside may not pass it — and never let a background write's failure mode be silence.** |
| **F-198**| **A cache hit reported the timing of the render that filled it, and a bypassed request reported a lookup that never happened**| **FIXED 2026-09-04 by A2 (P-135, `4360353f0`).** Two reporting defects in one surface, both the shape [[F-190]] and `entry-load` exist to prevent — **a measurement claiming a cost the request never paid.** First: with the Worker's cache working, hits came back carrying `landing-data;dur=2`, inherited from the response stored earlier; a hit performs **no data fetch at all**, so the line described work that did not occur. The unit test missed it because it asserted against a synthetic `Response` with no phases on it — it proved the stored copy carried no `Server-Timing` only because **nothing had put one there** ([[F-196]]'s shape again, in the same packet). Second: a request carrying a session came back `X-Supaprod-Cache: MISS`, which states the cache was consulted and empty; it was **never consulted**, and a reader checking whether sessions bypass the cache would have read MISS as proof they do not. **Fixes: the stored copy drops `Server-Timing` and `X-Supaprod-Timing`, so a hit reports only its own `worker-total`; and the marker carries three states — HIT, MISS, BYPASS — because "looked and found nothing" and "never looked" are different facts.** Verified against a fresh cache in workerd: MISS `landing-data;3, entry-load;67, worker-total;623`, then HIT `worker-total;15` and HIT `worker-total;3`, nothing else. |
| **F-199**| **The run screen's contract reader kept only STRINGS, and every spec in production stores OBJECTS — so "how we will know" was empty on all of them, silently**| **FIXED 2026-09-04 by A2 (P-137).** `components/track/spec-contract.ts` read `success_metrics` through a filter that kept only strings. **MEASURED on production: of the 16 specs carrying a contract, 16 store the clauses as an array of OBJECTS and none store strings** (`jsonb_typeof` on the array and its first element, grouped). So `specContract().measures` came back **empty for every spec that has ever existed**, and `ArtifactPane` — the run screen pane whose entire job is to say how anyone would know the work worked — rendered none of them, on all of them, with no error and no gap in the layout to notice. `OutcomeContractPanel` on the product surface reads the object shape correctly, so **two readers of one column disagreed and only the other one was right**, which is why nothing ever surfaced it: whichever screen you checked, the clauses were there. The reader was not narrower than the spec; it was narrower than the DATA, and it had been for as long as the column had objects in it. **The fix reads both shapes and keeps the string branch** (it costs one line, and a reader that only understands today's shape is precisely how this happened), leaves superseded clauses out because they no longer promise anything, and carries the clause objects through so no caller re-parses the jsonb a second time and drifts from the first reader. Sibling of [[F-192]] and [[F-196]]: a check that cannot see what production holds is not a check. |
| **F-200**| **Both of the first live release's metrics named an evidence source, both refs RESOLVED, and neither could produce a number — so every "has a source?" check answered yes**| **FIXED 2026-09-04 by A2 (P-137).** Spec `f2aa82f1`'s two outcome metrics (tablet checkout completion from 67 percent, address-screen abandonment from 41 percent) each carry `oracle_kind: "eval"` and an `oracle_ref` that points at a **real `eval_cases` row** — `a653a20b` and `6dbb1c54`, both in suite `1b27272f` "Spec acceptance: ...", created 2026-09-02. A predicate shaped *"does this metric name a source?"* returns true for both. **MEASURED: `eval_case_results` for each ref is 0, `product_analytics` for the workspace is 0 rows, and the 5 connections are github, linear, salesforce and slack — no analytics provider.** Neither case has ever run, and a spec-acceptance eval could not observe a customer's live checkout even if it had: it asserts OUR build satisfies the clause, not that THEIR users behaved differently. **A reference that resolves was standing in for a fact that is true** — the same shape as [[F-190]], [[F-192]], [[F-196]] and [[F-199]], and the reason it survived is that every layer that could have caught it was asking the checkable question instead of the true one. **The rule: the question is "could this produce a reading about the customer's product, now?", never "is the field populated?"** — and `ci` and `uat` are reported as the acceptance checks they honestly are rather than counted as sources, because a green build is not an observation of users and counting it would let Learn publish a graded outcome off CI. Consequence on the record: the spec's two real metrics have **no forecast row at all** (the only decision linked to the spec has a null horizon), so nothing would have graded them on 09-09 or 09-21; and decision `180fbac2`, the only Helio forecast due within six days, is the paperwork shape [[F-186]] already refuses. |
| **F-201**| **A batched credit read that fails partway reports a real, confident, WRONG total instead of an absence — a trace whose own events straddle two `.in()` batches shows only the batch that survived**| **FIXED 2026-09-04 by A3 (P-140).** Start's own diagnostic on the served payload (50 tracks, 174 traces, one `.in("trace_id", traceIds)` call) came back `creditsKeys: 0` with no error on the outer read, while the run screen's single-track call — same function, at most a few dozen traces — returned the real figure for the same data. **The one thing that differs is the size of the IN-list**: `knowledge-graph-view.functions.ts` already batches its own `.in()` calls at 25, in its own words, "so a PostgREST URL can never run long, even at the node cap"; 174 UUIDs in one GET query crossed whatever cap this account's infra enforces, PostgREST answered with an error, and `creditsSpentByTrace`'s own `if (eventsError ...) return {}` swallowed it without a trace. **The first fix (batch at 25, log and skip a failed batch) was itself wrong**, caught by A1 before it shipped: a trace's own `ai_events` never split across batches (each trace_id is queried in exactly one), but its EVENT ids can land in two different `credit_ledger` batches — if one succeeds and one fails, the surviving batch's rows sum to a real number that looks complete and is not. **MEASURED against a fixture built to force exactly that split**: a trace with two events at a 25/1 batch boundary, one succeeding at 5 credits and one failing at 7, reported nothing rather than the wrong `5`. **The fix: every trace touched by ANY failed batch — its own `ai_events` batch or any `credit_ledger` batch holding one of its event ids — is dropped from the result entirely**, even if some of its rows were read successfully; unread, never a sum of only the batches that worked. **The rule: when a read is split into batches for one reason (URL length), a partial failure must not silently become a partial-but-confident answer for whatever unit (here, a trace) has data split across the batch boundary** — the caller cannot tell a true small number from an undercounted one, so the safe default is absence, matching this surface's own existing "credits: null, never a fabricated zero" rule one level up. |
| **F-202** | **"Let Learn try again" on a horizon-deferred track releases the hold and leaves the deferral, so the run page says "runs again on its next turn" for seventeen days and the honest hold line is never recomposed** | **OPEN, P-151.** Measured 21:05 IST 09-04 on the live release track 2fdf93b6: before the press, `last_hold = needs-evidence`, `deferred_until = 2026-09-21`, the card showing the pre-P-144 sentence. `retryStation` (track.functions.ts) clears `last_hold`, `last_hold_because`, `attempts` and `station_drives` and never touches `deferred_until`; after the press the row read `last_hold = null`, `deferred_until = 2026-09-21`, no agent run, and the page read "You released it · It runs again on its next turn" above "Ready when you are", on work the sweep will not touch until the 21st. The driver's free re-hold path (`row.last_hold === "needs-evidence"` with the forecast not due) can only recompose the P-144 hold line if the hold is still set and the sweep reaches the row, and the press removes both conditions. A1 repaired the row by hand (hold restored, deferral cleared) so the next sweep re-holds it with the fresh sentence. | P-151: the press on a deferred track lifts the deferral and drives, so the driver looks again for free and re-defers with the fresh sentence; the release note says what the look found. |
| **F-203** | **The Worker's own cache took the public landing page down: `caches.default.match` rejected on the hosting runtime and, sitting before the handler's try, escaped as an unhandled 500** | **FIXED 2026-09-08 by Lane 1 (`280bb7f80`).** Seen 12:16 IST: every anonymous read of `/`, `/pricing`, `/demo` and `/security` answered HTTP 500 with the platform's raw `{"error":true,"status":500,"unhandled":true}` and **none of the Worker's headers on it** (no `x-frame-options`, no `x-supaprod-cache`, no `x-deployment-id`), which places the throw outside the handler's own try/catch. Three reads pinned it: the failing set was exactly `CACHEABLE_MARKETING_ROUTES` while `/film`, outside it, never failed; the same routes answered 200 with `x-supaprod-cache: BYPASS` the moment the lookup was skipped (`Cache-Control: no-cache`, or a session cookie); and the only code before the try on those routes was P-135's `await cacheStore.match(request)`. workerd, where P-135 was proved (F-197 probed `caches.default` there and it worked), does not throw here; the runtime that actually serves this (`x-deployment-id psr2...`, build canary null) hands back a store whose `match` rejects. Nothing pushed that day touched `server.ts`: **the runtime under the Worker changed, and a lookup with nowhere to land turned an optimisation into an outage.** Lane 2 read the same symptom as a landing-reader null bug from a run started at 12:10; the bypass reads on the same data disproved it in one curl. Fix: the lookup and the hit's re-wrap sit inside one catch (`answerFromEdgeCache`), a refused read is reported under surface `edge-cache-match` and treated as a miss; the write (`storeInEdgeCache`) is `Promise.resolve().then(put).catch(report)` so a put that throws synchronously cannot reach the handler's catch either; and a source guard pins that the fetch handler never calls `match` or `put` itself. **Deployed 12:31 IST as faa89898; all thirteen routes 200 by 12:33, and `error_events` then carried the runtime's own reason under `edge-cache-match`: "Cache API is not yet supported for dynamically-loaded workers."** Lovable loads the Worker dynamically now, so the Worker-held half of P-135 can never fill there; standing the store down after the first refusal (or retiring that half) is handed to Lane 3. The rule, F-197's sibling: **a cache may cost a render, never the page, and any I/O placed before the handler's try must be proved harmless on the runtime that serves, not the one that tests.** | P-135: the Worker holds its own marketing pages; this row is the failure mode that holding introduced. |
| **F-204** | **`parseDesignMd` ended every section at `\Z`, which JavaScript reads as a literal Z, so a Principles section naming a Zoom control lost everything after the Z** | **FIXED 2026-09-08 by Lane 3 (`6ca467cc8`)**, found by the lint rule no-useless-escape once P-152 pointed at it; `(?![\s\S])` is end of input, and a test puts a Z inside a section |
| **F-205** | **A person's Stop does not cancel the run's pending `agent_approvals`.** `cancelMission` did (`agent_approvals` → cancelled on a stop) and had no door since Board.tsx went (`aea9e40c7`); `stopTrack` (R-32) stamps `stop_requested_at` and the driver halts, and nothing on that path touches the approvals, so a stopped run's gate can stay on Waiting for a person to answer | Read during P-146 (Lane 3, 2026-09-08), `cancelMission` deleted with the gap recorded rather than carried as dead code | **OPEN.** The fix belongs on `stopTrack`'s path (or the driver's stop branch): cancel the run's pending approvals with the stop's own reason, and a guard that a stopped track has no pending gate |
| **F-206** | **No prototype can be made public.** `togglePrototypeShare` is the only writer of `prototypes.is_public` and has had no caller since `/decide` went (P-14); both writers of a prototype (`design.draft`, `publishPrototypeFromPrd`) insert `is_public: false`, and the pane's "Open full size" door (ArtifactPane) and `/p/$slug` both gate on it. So the public viewer is unreachable for every prototype in the product, and no surface says so | Read during P-146 (Lane 3, 2026-09-08) | **OPEN.** Either a share control on the Design artifact (Lane 2's pane; the writer exists) or the door and the route go. Kept on the unreachable list by name until decided |
| **F-207** | **The spine driver dispatches Build without the spec gate or the design gate.** `specGateBlocksDispatch` and `designGateBlocksDispatch` are enforced in exactly one place, `dispatchBuilderMission`, whose only caller (ReadyToBuild on /build) went with P-14; `driveTrackOnce` creates the Build mission through `createMission` and checks neither. Everywhere else the two are reads for display | Read during P-146 (Lane 3, 2026-09-08); `dispatchBuilderMission` kept on the unreachable list by name for this reason | **OPEN.** The driver's Build dispatch should ask both gates before it spends a seat, with the refusal on the hold card; R-30 says a branch commit runs without asking, not that an unapproved spec builds |
| **F-208** | **A held file claim on a run that never reached a terminal status has no manual exit.** Claims release on the run's terminal status (trigger), on cancel, on abandon and on merge; `releaseBuilderClaim`, the operator force-release, lost its door with /build, and the tool registry told agents to "release the claim from /build", a redirect stub | Read during P-146 (Lane 3, 2026-09-08); the registry's two sentences now say how a claim actually releases (`7980285de`) | **OPEN.** A release press on the run screen's claim hold (Lane 2's card; the writer exists), or a sweep that releases claims whose run has been silent past the stall ceiling |
| **F-209** | **`getTrackActivity` answered every failed read, including a refused runs read, as `turns: []`, which the transcript draws as "Nothing is recorded against this work yet"** | **FIXED 2026-09-08 by Lane 3 (`414bbf565`)** on Lane 2's live walk (a running seat invisible for fifty seconds; the read and RLS both admit the row, so the empty state was the only other path): a refused read throws with its reason and the catch re-throws; `useTrackActivityPush` invalidates the transcript on the track's own agent_runs rows, focus or not |
| **F-210** | **A fresh signup never saw the first run screen: `/start` was exempt from the onboarding gate from the days it was "the zero-config entry", and it is now where a signup lands** | **FIXED 2026-09-08 by Lane 1 (`6995c805f`).** Found by the entry review's first-visit lens and confirmed in `src/routes/_authenticated.tsx`: the gate redirected a `firstRun` account to `/onboarding` from every route except `/start`, and the shell drew `/start` chromeless for a `firstRun` account instead. Since the home became `/start` and `SIGNED_IN_HOME` is where the sign-in lands, a new account went straight to an empty chromeless home and the one-screen FirstRun (the product's own first impression, built the same morning) was unreachable. Nothing pinned the exemption; the two tests that mentioned "zero-config" were about other things. Fix: every route gates; the chromeless branch went with the exemption; FirstRun opens the home itself. The rule: **when a route changes meaning (a side door becomes the front door), grep for every exemption written for its old meaning; an exemption outlives the reason it was written for and nothing fails when it does.** | The entry, Lane 1 (2026-09-08): FirstRun, the home, the gate. |
| **F-211** | **The home classified a run with a private list of "held" reasons that disagreed with the driver's sets, so a person's call drew a grey wait and a final stop drew Decide over an empty card** | **FIXED 2026-09-08 by Lane 1 (`6995c805f`).** `journey-of-a-run.ts` kept its own `HELD` set; the driver decides whose a hold is with `holdTone` and whether the loop will move it again with `nothingIsComing`, and the run screen's `runNow` reads those two. On Prism, "the-call-is-yours" (tone you, not in the private set) drew a neutral wait with no control, and "corrections-spent" (tone you, nothing coming, in the private set) drew Decide, which opened `HoldCard`, which renders nothing for that register. Fix: `standingState` reads the same two predicates (a calendar wait still first and still neutral), a person's call with no gate under it gets Answer that opens the run, and the census guard `one-place-says-whose-move-it-is.test.ts` names the new reader. The rule, F-197's cousin on the client: **a copy of a classification the driver owns is a second writer of one fact; import the predicate, never the list, and let the census guard name every reader.** | The home's rows and hero; see [[a-private-copy-of-a-driver-set-drifts]] in the session memory. |
| **F-212** | **The Inbox's queue read took 7.7 s and the database was innocent: twelve sequential Worker-to-PostgREST round trips, seven of them inside a nested server function** | **FIXED 2026-09-08 by Lane 3 (`996ab01dc`, `5f6487d90`).** Lane 2 timed `getApprovalsQueue` at 7,694 ms on Helio Labs. Every query it runs was then timed as the signed-in user with RLS on: 0.05 to 6 ms each, about 20 ms for the lot, and production `pg_stat_statements` agreed (the authenticated role's worst statement on these tables ever was 262 ms). The handler awaited twelve reads one after another, and `listGovernApprovals`, called as a nested server function that re-ran the auth middleware, held seven of them. Per hop this deployment pays ~275 ms warm / ~550 ms cold (measured on `landing-data`, a 0.03 ms query). Fix: the four nested readers became plain functions called with the request's own client (`readGovernApprovals`, `readDecisions`, `readHouseRules`, `readTrustGraduationProposals`); reads that key off the same row leave together; the assumption challenges carry their assumption, decision and spec as PostgREST embeds; the decisions read carries the mission's status and the spec's project so the dedup and project hops are gone; two hops on the scoped path, three in one rare case, guarded by `a-queue-is-two-hops-deep.test.ts` with a fake client that counts rounds. The same census then took the strip's read (F-213), `listRunningNow` five hops to two (`5a6b00615`), `countNeedsYouCalls` two to one (`a06a5c4c4`), `getTrackActivity` three to two (`44ba22a50`), the home's "live since" chain through a `deployments` embed (`3fea10214`) and `creditsSpentByTrace` to one SQL call (`b663d059d`, migration `20260909100600`), and the run screen's transcript to one SQL call (`e8c062d27`, `track_tool_calls`, migration `20260909100700`: three hops and 240 KB to one hop and 86 KB on a 224-call track). The 170 KB / 5 s call on every page turned out to be `listMissions` through `useLiveAgents` and the shell's marks, not the transcript: `mission_marks` (`212153b00`, migration `20260909100800`) answers the eight fields those readers use in one hop and about 7 KB. That was not the 170 KB either: it was `getMission`, called per mission by the run screen for its handoff rows alone (`382a72682`: `listMissionHandoffs`, one hop; `getMission` three hops from nine on `latest_run_checkpoints`, migration `20260909100900`, never shipping a checkpoint's whole state or a run's `input`). The lesson for the next reader: a server function's id in the network log is named by the built chunk, not by its size or timing. Read live on 2e038667: handler 995 to 1,519 ms by its own Server-Timing entry; Lane 2 saw the cards at 3,163 ms against about ten seconds before. The page also fired the whole queue once per OTHER workspace for the "N waiting in X" line (21 calls on one load); `approvals_queue_counts` (migration 20260909100500, SECURITY INVOKER) answers that in one round trip, 53 both ways on Helio Labs. The rule: **a slow read on this Worker is almost never the query; count the sequential awaits, including the ones inside nested server functions, and put a Server-Timing entry on the handler so the next reading can split handler time from the Worker's own.** | The Inbox, the home (`readHome`), the shell's count, the briefing and loop-state, which all call this read. |
| **F-213** | **The shell strip's read (`listStudioSessions`) spent 3.7 to 3.9 s inside the Worker on every authenticated page: eleven serial hops** | **FIXED 2026-09-08 by Lane 3 (`3be14ea45`).** Same disease as F-212, found on the same page's Server-Timing (`worker-total=3734`, `3856`). The handler awaited builder runs, then other runs, then proposed missions, then a four-way Promise.all, then studio_changes, then prds, then checkpoints and ai_events once per run kind, then agents. `readStudioSessions` is three hops now: the three reads that need only the caller leave together; everything keyed on the run rows leaves together (the checkpoint trace read once for both kinds); everything keyed on those leaves together. The two run kinds keep their own read, page and cost tally, which OBS-10 recorded as an adversarial-review finding. Guarded by `a-strip-read-is-three-hops-deep.test.ts` on the shared wire (`src/__tests__/a-wire-that-counts-rounds.ts`). Read live on c52a04db (pressed 21:55 IST): `worker-total=1502` on the strip's call by its own Server-Timing, and Lane 2's cold read put the strip's handlers between 0.85 and 1.56 s against 3.7 to 3.9 s before. What remains on a cold Worker is outside the handlers (a 4.5 s resource carried `worker-total` 1.2 s): the dynamically loaded Worker's own start, which no handler can shorten. | Every page's strip; the Build board. |
| **F-214** | **A signup was held on a disabled "Open Supaprod" for up to twelve seconds while the machine wrote its starter runs, invisibly** | **FIXED 2026-09-08 by Lane 3 (`4d50df6ac`), from Lane 1's third review.** `completeOnboarding` awaited the generation bounded at 12 s, and `listStarterRuns` held its own 12 s on a miss. Both answer at once now; the generation runs behind the response after a claim on the row (`starter_runs_at`, honoured for `STARTER_RUNS_CLAIM_MS`, taken by a conditional update so two writers cannot generate the same row); the home's read shows the wait as the agent's first visible work. Because a Worker may cancel a promise once its response is out, the resume-runs minute sweep is the guarantee: a product from the last day with nothing stored and no live claim is generated there, two per pass, under the product's own user. `starterRunsState` (pure) decides ready / refused / in-flight / unclaimed for every reader; `keepStarterRuns` keeps a refusal wherever the generation ran. The rule: **never hold a response for a model; claim, kick, answer pending, and let a sweep finish what a cancelled isolate dropped.** | FirstRun, the home's starter runs, the sweep. |
| **F-215** | **The header named the queue "decisions" while its own tooltip, the hero, the run rows and the driver said "calls", and on the home it followed "Last finished" with the title of a track waiting on a call** | **FIXED 2026-09-08 by Lane 1 (`a19c75fdb`), superseding the round-three refutation.** The first pass of round three kept "decisions" on the 2026-08-10 comment (the market's word, measured at 562.8 per million). The verifier's second pass showed the stronger mechanism: `gateCount` reads `listGatesOnTracks`, whose own code calls them boundary calls; on Inbox the header's "N decisions" sat above `shapeSentence`'s "N decisions" for a different population, two numbers under one word; and positioning spends "decision" on the decision record, which is a reason not to spend it on every pending tool call. One noun for the queue now: call. The same commit closes the home's half: `liveLead` skipped the gate on the board but `liveFacts` and `liveTarget` did not, so with a gate waiting and nothing moving the strip read "Last finished · <a waiting track's title>" with a door titled Open Inbox; both skip on the board now. The rule: **a word pinned by a comment is not a ruling; when two counts of two populations share a noun on one screen, the noun is the defect.** | The shell's live line, on every page; `the-bar-counts-gates-on-open-tracks.test.ts` pins the count, not the spelling. |
| **F-216** | **The home's largest read, `listRunsForStart` at 3.3 to 5.3 s cold, ran four times on one arrival** | **FIXED 2026-09-08 by Lane 1 (`6a9c54b26`), from Lane 2's Server-Timing read of 7187cb60.** The layout loader prefetched it; `readHome` fetched it again inside the composite; then both observers on `["start-runs", ws]` refetched on mount, because a row seeded with `setQueryData` is stale the instant it lands under TanStack's default stale time of zero; and every realtime row cancelled whatever was in flight and started it over, so a burst of updates could keep the read from ever finishing. Fix: `home-read.ts` is the one module that knows the composite read and the four keys it seeds; the loader prefetches `readHome` (the arrival still starts before the onboarding gate resolves, and the home joins that promise); the seed is fresh for one poll interval on both observers; the two pushes invalidate with `cancelRefetch: false`, and the poll carries what the flight missed. That was half of it: read live on 72c04f6e with a fetch hook, `readHome` ran once from the loader and the runs read still ran three more times (the server function that accepts a null workspace is `listRunsForStart`, which is how the two had been mislabelled). The shell's queue and seats reads and the run list mounted while the composite was still out and fetched 170 ms before the seed landed, which no stale time can prevent while the cache is empty; the run list first read a null workspace; and both realtime channels refetched every seeded key on their FIRST SUBSCRIBED, two seconds after the rows were read. Second half (`822c0d7a4`): `useSeedInFlight` (an observer of a seeded key waits while the composite is out; off the home nothing waits), the home seeded only for a known workspace with `YourRuns` mounted onto the seed, and the pushes refetching on a reconnect only. Read live on f105b0dd at 01:47 IST 09-09: `readHome` once at 1.5 s, no standalone runs read, the queue once, 34 server calls on the arrival against 46. The rule: **a seeded key is not a warm key until it carries a stale time and its observers wait for the seed in flight; an invalidation that cancels is a restart; and the first connect of a socket has no gap to close.** | The home's arrival. |
| **F-217** | **The shell's idle line read "Ready for the first run" over a run waiting on the person, because it read missions and a run is a mission only from Build** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 1 (`7ea9db5d0`), fourth review.** `lastDone` was the newest finished mission, and the spine creates a mission only at Build (`driver.server.ts`), so a workspace whose runs stood at Discover, Decide or Plan had none: the header said no run existed while the hero 120 px below said "1 run needs you" and the mark beside the words said "asking". The newest open run, from the open-tracks read the header already polls, is the idle fact when it moved after the last finish ("Last moved · <title> · 12m ago", door onto that run); the first-run invitation is reachable only when no run exists. Same commit: the facts under a live seat's sentence named the last finished mission as if it were the seat's run (the facts follow the lead's order now); the mission path compared a catalog name to a slug and never matched, so every Build run read "is working" with no verb and no quiet suffix (the seat carries its slug and is joined by its run first); a mission branch named one agent while the strip showed two (the seats a mission does not cover are counted, "· 1 more"); the presence read's failure reached neither the line nor the mark; the mark breathed "working" over a seat every other surface called quiet (a still `quiet` state from the seats still calling). The rule: **the header is the product's always-on claim about what is happening, and every branch of it must read the same seats, the same clock and the same failure the surfaces under it read.** | The shell on every page. |
| **F-218** | **Two numbers of one noun forty pixels apart: the header counted gates on open tracks while the Inbox row beside it counted the queue** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 1 (`7ea9db5d0`), superseding P-18a's half about the sentence.** P-18a took the queue out of the sentence because its count was bounded per family and named a number nobody could find; F-212 made the queue count exact, and the row already read it (a row's count is its page's count, P-56). One number in the shell now, the queue's, with the track gates as the floor for the beat before the queue answers; the sentence, the mark and the row read it, and the counts test says so in its own title. Also: the line's door resolved to the page the person was on (Inbox on Inbox, the run on its own screen, the home on the home); doors carry their address and resolve once, and a door onto the current page is the composer on the home and a statement elsewhere. The rule: **when two counts of two populations share a noun on one screen, the noun is the defect, and a door to the room you are standing in is not a door.** | The shell's live line and the rail. |
| **F-219** | **The home painted a terminal hold as an amber condition, said "1 run is moving" over a quiet seat, rounded an unread queue to an all-clear, and drew the day-one promise over a failed runs read** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 1's home agent (`8fbc07303`), fourth review, fourteen findings.** A tone-you hold with nothing coming is the person's (the driver and the run screen said so; the home painted `held`): a `stopped` state in the you hue, no breath, on the home and the run screen. The hero counts a quiet seat ("1 run has gone quiet. Its seat has not moved for 45 min"), distinguishes an unread queue from zero (the all-clear only on an answered queue, `ReadFailedLine` with retry on a failed one), says "Your runs could not be read" instead of the first-visit promise on a failed read, and holds the composer's placeholder until its subject is known (it changed sentence up to three times on arrival). The map's working stop stops its clock, breath and dot together on a quiet seat; a held row's control no longer wears a station's name ("Why it stopped", not "Decide"); a pinned row's "First" is a record tag, not a you-hue chip; the morning after the only run finished stays a map, not the promise; the composer's failure receipt states only what the client knows and refreshes the list; a slow read gets a retry. New guards: `a-pin-is-not-a-call`, `the-stop-s-clock-stops-with-its-breath`. Also found: `hero-copy.test.ts` had never run (it called `test` without importing it). | The home and FirstRun. |
| **F-220** | **On a 390px phone a run row's title got 68px, the phone bar's live line floated above its doors with the page scrolling through the slit, and the header offered "Ask ⌘K" as a 34px target** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 1's phone agent (`663808eac`), fourth review.** A fixed 168px time column stayed inside the row body after the control dropped under: below `sm` the time folds under the sentence, indented to the text (title 249px, was 68). The phone bar is one fixed box, its line 44px like its doors, its height a Meridian token the page's padding reads (`--mrd-shell-phone-bar-h`), guarded by `the-phone-bar-and-its-clearance-share-one-number`. Keycaps hide on a coarse pointer and Ask, the scope and the account disc get 44px targets. The vertical road meets its node centres. The phone rail draws the desktop's glyphs and the Inbox count. The rail's Working-now row is no longer a door to the run the person is already reading. | The phone shape of the home, the shell and the rail. |
| **F-221** | **"Open Supaprod" was seven sequential authenticated server-function hops, so the first press in the product sat disabled for seconds; and a transient provider error at signup was stored as a permanent refusal that nothing retried** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 3 (`f812cba50`) and Lane 1 (`bae976611`), fourth review.** `openFirstRun` runs the name and the seed together, everything keyed on the seed together, then the completion, on the request's own client (F-212's shape); FirstRun presses it once. `keepStarterRuns` keeps a refusal only for a genuine empty answer, in a sentence for the person; any other throw leaves the row NULL with the claim re-stamped so the sweep retries after the two-minute window. The four server functions the one call replaced are orphans by name in the unreachable baseline until Lane 3 retires them. The rule: **the first press in the product is measured in hops like any other read, and an error that will pass is never written as a refusal that will not.** | FirstRun, the starter runs. |
| **F-222** | **Every sentence typed on the home was filed as new work and walked all seven stations, so "fix the broken login" opened a Discover run** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 1 (`495aae117`), fifth review.** `shape` was the constant `"new-capability"` at the home's one start call and at the Ask pane's, and only a ranked bet or an example card ever carried a shape, so four of the five routes the model knows were unreachable from either front door. `suggestRoute` has given each shape an entry station and its policy waivers for months (`incident-fix` enters at Build with four stations waived), and the founder's own §5E ruling asked for the shorter route to be "a first-class selectable route now... Do not make it the default". `ComposerRoutePicker` sits beside the product picker in the same mute one-line register: the person picks in their own words ("Something is broken now"), the machine answers in its own ("it enters at Build; Discover, Decide, Plan and Design are waived"), and the clause is derived from `suggestRoute` rather than written beside it, so a waiver changed in `route.ts` changes the sentence in the same edit. Discover-first stays the default. The rule: **a default the product has never let a person see is not a default, it is a guess made on their behalf; say the route before the press and let them name the work in their own words.** | The home's composer. **The Ask pane's half is closed too** (verified 2026-09-09 afternoon): `AskPane.tsx` passes `shape: shapeOfTheWork(text)` and carries the origin, and the sentence a person reads is derived from `suggestRoute(shape).entry` through the one station map, so neither front door holds a literal shape any more. |
| **F-223** | **Findings claimed the person's move on every new workspace's first visit** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 1 (`495aae117`), fifth review.** The headline "N clusters need your decisions" draws only when `leadOfRanking` returns null, which is exactly when the top-ranked cluster is an Example, which is every seeded workspace's first visit; one press away the hero read "Nothing you started is waiting on you". Clusters are promoted unattended by the sweep, so an open cluster is the machine's state and not a call: the fallback now reads "N clusters are open, ranked." The rejected half of the same finding is recorded here so it is not refiled: **do not** give clusters a family in `getApprovalsQueue`, the rail badge or the hero's shape, because counting them would make three surfaces claim a block that does not exist. | Findings, first visit. |
| **F-224** | **A call and the run that raised it are not linked in any table, and a track's `pending_gates` is never cleared, so one run on the founder's own workspace has been "waiting on a person" for nineteen days over an approval that expired** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 3, measured by Lane 1 against production, from a round-five finding and a first measurement by Lane 2.** Round five filed "a call reaches the Inbox without its run" and proposed resolving pending gate ids against open tracks' `pending_gates`. Lane 2 measured that join before building it and found the six ids matched no row in eight artifact tables. Re-measured: **the ids are not missing, they are settled `agent_approvals` rows** (4 expired, 1 cancelled, 1 executed) still listed as pending on their track, which is bug one: `pending_gates` is written when a gate is raised and never cleared when it settles, so track `197769e8` on Helio Labs has held at `waiting-on-a-person` since 21 August over a gate no surface can ever show, and five more are the same shape one hold along. Bug two is larger and is the founder's "the layers do not stitch together" in its measurable form: **nothing links a pending call to its run by any path.** Of 21 pending approvals, 0 appear in any track's `pending_gates`; 14 carry a `run_id`, all 14 match a real `agent_runs` row, and **0 of those rows carry a `track_id`, while 2,732 `agent_runs` rows do**; `trace_id` reaches a track for 0 of 21, `mission_id` for 0 of 21. Every pending call in the product was created on 24 or 25 July by `backlog.prioritize`, `mission.dispatch` and `memory.promote`: the queue's tool_call family is entirely pre-spine. So the proposed fix could not have resolved one item in either direction, and `spine_track_members.artifact_id` is the only join with any reach (about 20 of 636). **Both halves fixed.** Why nobody could clear the stale gates: the prune is correct and lives in the driver's harvest, which runs only inside a drive, and four of the six are held at `station-cannot-finish`, which the tick excludes as terminal by design, so **the hold hid the record that says the hold is over**. `repairStaleGates` runs in the minute sweep: it harvests and prunes, drives nothing (a terminal hold stays terminal, a paused workspace is untouched), and clears exactly one hold, `waiting-on-a-person` with nothing left pending, whose claim is false once the gate is settled. And the link was **already on the write**: the loop stamps `agent_approvals.run_id` on every gate it raises and `agent_runs.track_id` on every spine run, and nothing read it; the govern read now resolves run to track inside the hop it already makes, and `ApprovalQueueItem` carries `trackId`, null per family for the pre-spine calls. **Verified on production after the press** (deployment `c256c12b`, served 04:10:32 UTC): tracks carrying a settled gate went 6 to 1 and `waiting-on-a-person` 1 to 0 within two sweeps, so `197769e8`'s nineteen-day false wait is over. The last one sat outside the repair's window because `pending_gates` defaults to `[]` and the read filtered on "is not null", matching all 74 open tracks; excluding the empty array narrows the window to tracks a pass can help. One reading error worth keeping: the first "after" was taken 2.5 minutes and one sweep after the build served, not thirteen, because it was measured from the BEFORE rather than from the deployment; the database answers in UTC and the deploy log is in IST. The rule: **measure a join before building on it, and check the table the id was written by, not only the tables you expect it to name.** | The Inbox, the run screen's calls, the home's stopped runs, the driver's holds. |
| **F-225** | **Reduced motion stopped no transition, under either switch, so every `transition-colors`, fold and fade in the product ran at full duration with motion off** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 1 (`1e7ba1e46`), from Lane 2's craft pass.** Lane 2 reported the in-product toggle as the weaker half of a pair, the OS twin carrying a transition stop it did not. Measured before writing the fix: **neither block stopped a transition.** Both name keyframes only, and the sole transition stop in the file was one local rule on the scroll fade, so the OS setting was as ineffective as ours. The law had already decided the case: "if stopping it removes a fact, it is not decoration" is why an elapsed timer keeps ticking, and a transition removes no fact when you stop it, because it has one defined end state and stopping it means arriving there at once. Both blocks stop it now at `transition-duration: 1ms` rather than `none`, because `none` cancels `transitionend` and nothing in the repo listens for one **today**, which is a trap for whoever adds the first listener rather than a reason to use `none`. `transition-delay` goes to 0 in the same rule: a stopped transition that still waits looks frozen rather than fast. The rule: **when a report names one half of a pair as broken, measure the other half before you trust it as the reference.** | Every surface, under both motion switches. |
| **F-226** | **Twenty-one bare transitions on the run screen, three speeds among them, none on Meridian's easing** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 1 (`1e7ba1e46`), Lane 2's finding and Lane 2's own preferred remedy.** `transition-colors` with no duration takes Tailwind's 150ms and its default easing, both outside the ramp (`--mrd-d-press` 120ms, `--mrd-d-move` 140ms, `--mrd-d-enter` 420ms, `--mrd-ease` out-quint). The choice was a sweep of the twenty-one call sites or two theme variables; the sweep fixes those twenty-one and none of the next twenty-one. Meridian sets `--default-transition-duration` and `--default-transition-timing-function` in `@theme inline`, so every unqualified transition in the product is correct at once and stays correct. `--mrd-d-press` is the default rather than `--mrd-d-move` because the bare utilities are overwhelmingly `transition-colors` on a control, which is the press budget by definition; anything moving names a duration and still wins, since a utility with a value outranks the default. The rule: **when a defect is a missing default, set the default; sweeping call sites treats the instances and leaves the cause.** | Every bare `transition-*` in the product. |
| **F-227** | **A live seat's halo peaked at nearly three times its own value, at two call sites, because it borrowed keyframes tuned for an element with no resting opacity** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 1 (`1e7ba1e46`), from Lane 2's measurement of one of the two.** `mrd-attention` drives opacity from 1 at 0% and 100%, which is correct for `StatusChip`: the chip sets no resting opacity, so the keyframes' 1 IS its normal value. `AgentPresence`'s halo sets `opacity: 0.35` and the animation OVERRODE it, so the element whose whole job is to be quietly present peaked near 1.0. Checking the other users of the keyframe found the same defect on the run road's working ring at `opacity: 0.55`, which Lane 2 did not have; `Character.tsx` is correctly on `mrd-attention` and says so, its mark sets no resting opacity. The new `mrd-halo` **reads the resting value rather than naming one** (`var(--mrd-halo-rest, 0.35)` and `calc(... * 0.32)`, the ratio `mrd-attention` was tuned to), because a block hard-coding 0.35 would have fixed the first call site and quietly dimmed the second, which is how a system acquires a third animation. It is in both motion lists and the parity test passes. The rule: **an animation that SETS a property it did not declare will override whatever the element was tuned to; an envelope reads the value and scales it.** | The rail's live seats, the run road's working node. |
| **F-228** | **The longest document in the product rendered with no measure: `Prose` capped its non-markdown branch only** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 1 (`1e7ba1e46`), from Lane 2's craft pass.** The markdown branch dropped the panel, the pre-wrap, the outer margin **and the measure**, ported wholesale from the legacy `.sp-prose[data-markdown]` on the reasoning that "inside Ask the pane IS the measure". True of Ask and of nowhere else: the spec body renders through the same branch on a pane wide enough to run the full width. Both branches carry it now, and on the rem rung (`--mrd-measure-prose`, 32rem) rather than `68ch`, because a ch is the zero's advance width and the same number is a different column in every font. Ask is unaffected, since a pane narrower than the rung was never capped by it. The rule: **a measure is a property of reading, not of the container it happens to sit in; the component carries it and a caller that wants full bleed overrides it.** | Every `Prose markdown` block, the spec body most of all. |
| **F-229** | **`.mrd-focus` was named on ten elements and defined nowhere** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 1 (`1e7ba1e46`), from Lane 2's craft pass, which correctly declined to churn the ten call sites.** It was a no-op rather than a missing ring: the treatment comes from `[data-mrd][data-mrd] :focus-visible`, so all ten were correctly ringed and nothing looked wrong. That is exactly why it needed settling rather than leaving, and it is the same shape as the retired-linker rule in `route-inventory.test.ts`: **a thing that looks like coverage and is not is worse than an absence, because nobody goes looking.** Defined rather than retired, beside `.mrd-focus-inset`, because the pair is the point: the inset variant is meaningful only against a default it can be read as differing from, and choosing between them at a call site is the real decision (does this control sit inside something that clips). It restates the offset deliberately, so the pair is one edit away from being re-tuned together. Same commit records the defect the specificity bump caused, which Lane 2 found and fixed: three controls declared `focus-visible:outline-offset-[-2px]` and drew OUTSET, because the attribute rule outranks a Tailwind variant on elements carrying `data-mrd` themselves. | Ten controls across Lane 2's surfaces. |
| **F-230** | **The run screen, the most important surface in the product, titles one rung BELOW every other page in it** | **RULED 2026-09-09 by Lane 1, built by Lane 2 (`402086098`).** The finding was filed as "the largest type on the route is reserved for its failure states", `PageHeading` at 25px against the run's `<h1>` at 20px. **The premise is wrong:** `PageHeading` has one code path and sets `text-mrd-h2` on EVERY title it draws, with no failure variant, so it is not a failure state being styled louder than a working one. The real defect is that the run screen sets its `<h1>` in `mrd-title`, which is the COMPONENT rung: dialogs, empty regions and cards use it. Ruled against both proposed fixes. **Not** raising the run title to 25px, because a run's name is a long sentence and 25px shouts on every visit, which is not calm. **Not** bringing `PageHeading` down, because 25px is right for a page title and the other route was never the one out of step. The diagnosis instead: **a run's name is not a title, it is a lead paragraph wearing a heading's clothes**, and setting a sentence one rung down does not fix the category error, it only makes the sentence quieter. So the run screen takes the shape `PageHeading` models rather than the component (its header carries a `StatusChip` and `PageHeading` has no slot for one): the station eyebrow above, drawn with the glyph the road draws, then the sentence as prose on the reading measure. Lane 2 built it and made one call worth recording: **there is no short page title, because a run has no short label**, so the sentence remains the `<h1>` and the eyebrow is not promoted, on the ground that a heading element is the page's name in the document outline rather than its largest type, and naming the page "Build" would be true of a hundred runs. The rule: **before ruling on a type inversion, read what the component actually sets; a finding's premise is a claim about the reader.** | The run screen's header; the shape is a candidate Meridian part once it has been seen live. |
| **F-231** | **The shell stated an exact count of what is waiting over a read that reports its own gaps, on the one surface that never adopted the qualifier written for it** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 1 (`c66ec1c31`), from Lane 3's report of the sibling defect in the briefing.** P-18a retired the "at least" floor and was right at the time: the count came from `listGatesOnTracks`, one query over open tracks with no families to bound. **F-212 then made `getApprovalsQueue` the count** and left the old read as the fallback for the beat before the queue answers, which is precisely the read the floor was written for: ten families each bounded at a fixed limit, 116 specs pending a design gate against a limit of 100 when it was measured. The line prefers the bounded read now and the qualifier that belonged to it was not brought back with it, so `AppFrame` has been reading `queue.data.items.length` and never `queue.data.incomplete`. **Both other surfaces that state this count already say it**: `/approvals` and the home both call `notTheWholeQueue`; the shell was the one that never adopted it. Conditional, never blanket: the floor applies only when the queue is actually the source AND it reports a gap, so the fallback keeps its exact count. The number is not hidden, because it is still the most useful thing in the line and only its exactness was unearned; the rail's badge keeps its digit, because a mark that changes shape when a read degrades is noise on the surface that most needs to stay calm, and the sentence forty pixels away carries the caveat for both. The guard pins the pair AND the memo dependency, which is the half the morning's loading guard got wrong. The rule, **which cost three findings in one day** (this, F-217's guard, F-227's keyframes): **when a branch's source changes, grep for every qualifier written for the old source; a qualifier outlives its reason and nothing fails when it does.** | The shell's live line, on every page. |
| **F-232** | **The live line said "Ready for the first run" over a workspace whose name had not loaded, because a read made load-bearing that morning was never added to the guard** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 1 (`4e799a414`), caught on a cold load of the served build rather than by any gate.** F-217 made the newest OPEN RUN the idle fact, which made `openTracks` load-bearing for the sentence, and the loading guard still named only `missions` and `runningNow`. A workspace whose missions and seats answer empty in the first beat therefore fell through to the first-run invitation while the read that would have contradicted it was still out. Every test passed, because the code did exactly what it said. The guard names every read the sentence stands on now, and the test asserts that list and that it sits above the all-clear. | The shell on every page, on every cold arrival. |
| **F-233** | **One object, two nouns, 250 pixels apart on the most important screen in the product: the road said "10 drawings" and the story beside it said "filed 10 prototypes"** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 1 (`1c866d663`), caught by looking at the served build rather than by any gate.** `KIND_WORD` in `lib/spine/attach.ts` is the canon and says prototype; nine call sites read it, including Lane 2's new What-happened list. Four files carried a local swap to "drawing": `what-it-made.ts` mapped it by hand, `run-journey.ts` and `what-each-station-did.ts` each hard-coded the pair, and `live-station.ts` held it as a bare noun in a map whose every other entry is a noun and a past participle. **Prototype wins, and not only because it is the canon: "drawing" MISDESCRIBES the thing.** The locked positioning calls it "an interactive prototype a person can click before anyone writes code" and rests a differentiator on exactly that ("their preview saves rework; ours prevents it"), so calling it a drawing undersells the most distinctive artifact the loop files, and it is the founder's own outward word besides. The four swaps are deleted rather than reconciled, and the argument lives where the override used to be so the next author meets it. `live-station.ts` also stops being the one bare noun among its siblings: "prototype drafted", the same shape as "spec drafted" beside it. The nine assertions that changed were pinning this exact noun, which is what they were for, so updating them is the change landing rather than a baseline widening; `versions-of-one-thing.test.ts` passes the word in as a parameter and is untouched, and `artifact-words.ts` maps `prd_scaffold`, a different kind, and keeps its own word. The rule, which is F-215's mirror: **F-215 was two populations sharing one noun; this is one population wearing two nouns. Either way the noun is the defect, and a local swap of a canon word is a second writer of one fact.** | The run screen's road and its What-happened list; the home's journey line; the live station verb. |
| **F-234** | **Every value statement on the entry was a delta, and the founder's complaint is about the whole: nothing on the landing page said the loop had ever closed** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 1 (`b620f8410`), after two structural passes at the entry that day had both left it standing.** Measured rather than argued: his workspace holds 67 decisions, 38 specs, 37 prototypes, 12 graded outcomes and 8 deployments, and the entry said two sentences about any of it, in the smallest type on the page, both scoped to *since you last looked*, under a headline counting what he owes the machine. Closing the loop is the last four sevenths of the product's own claim (*tells you what to build, then builds it, ships it, grades it, guides the next call*) and the entry showed the first three and never the fourth. **`WhetherItWorked` draws ONE outcome**: what was decided, the verdict as a word in its own hue, the grader's own sentence carrying what was committed to and what came back, what the record did about it, and one door. On his workspace: *Decline shipping of "Improve onboarding flow based on user feedback signals" work · missed · Spec required 5% abandonment on the tablet address re-confirm screen within 7 days of full rollout. Actual outcome: tablet checkout completion is 67%, meaning ~33% abandonment, far above target.* **One and not twelve**, because a row of totals IS the dump of data and content he named and it makes the reader do the reading; twelve is inventory and one commitment against one result is evidence. **A miss leads as readily as a win**: nothing filters the verdict, because a surface that shows only its wins is marketing and a reader works that out by the third visit. Three rules it holds to: the grader's sentence is not rewritten (a second wording of one verdict is how two surfaces come to disagree); a seed row says so in WORDS rather than in a quieter colour (a colour still reads as his own result, and this repo has paid for that once); and a read that failed draws nothing, on its own null like the three answers beside it. It rides the home-answers read and key, so no new request (F-216's lesson). **Two earlier rulings were NOT overturned and both are still right:** the headline stays the count, and the three answers stay plain sentences. The gap was never their size, it was that none of them was evidence. One guard of mine failed for the right reason and is recorded in the file: the first version grepped the whole source for `pulse` and tripped on the comment saying the chip does not pulse, so it reads the chip and not the prose. The rule: **a delta answers "what changed"; only a commitment measured against a result answers "did it work", and a product whose whole claim is owning outcomes must say the second one on its front page.** | The entry, directly under the three answers. |
| **F-235** | **The home claimed "and the record was re-scored" over a decision whose record had not moved, and the new evidence region found it within an hour of shipping** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 1 (`c4fdce917`).** The clause was part of `learnedAnswer`'s sentence unconditionally, for every graded decision, and nothing in that read ever looked at a score. Measured on the founder's own workspace: the one decision whose forecast resolved this week carries `prior_ice` and `new_ice` both null, so nothing was re-scored and the entry said it was. **It was visible only because F-234's region sits directly under that line and shows the SAME decision**, and its own re-score line was absent because there was nothing to report: two statements about one decision, ninety pixels apart, disagreeing, and neither wrong-looking alone. The re-score lives on `learnings` and not on `decisions`, so it is its own head count rather than a field on the one already there; a row counts only when BOTH scores are present, the same rule the region's own line holds to, because half a re-score is not a movement. A null count withholds the clause rather than guessing in either direction. The failing test was pinning the old wording with one argument, so it now pins both branches and the withheld one, which is the claim rather than the spelling. The rule: **a surface that states a consequence must read the consequence; a clause welded to a sentence is an assertion nobody is checking, and the fastest way to find one is to put a second, independent statement of the same fact next to it.** | The home's three answers, and the evidence region under them. |
| **F-236** | **A control was painted across another control on every run row of the home** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 1 (`f643254b0`), seen on the served build.** The dark "Answer on the run" button drew over "Put first" and covered the first two letters of its word, on three consecutive rows. The control slot was a fixed 176px holding two controls and a gap, and the widest real pair needs about 204; `minmax(0, 1fr)` lets the first COLUMN shrink and does nothing to the button inside it, so the button kept its width and overflowed into its neighbour. The fixed width was written for a rule worth keeping (controls stand in the same place down the list whether or not a row has one), so it becomes a **floor rather than a cap**: rows that fit still land on the same 176, the wide pair takes the room it needs, and because the slot sits at the row's end the right edges stay aligned either way. The rule: **a control drawn across another control is worse than a misaligned one, which is the trade a fixed width quietly makes; size a fixed slot by its widest real pair or make it a minimum.** Same family as the clamp that never clamped: two properties fighting over one box, visible only on the served page. | The home's run rows. |
| **F-237** | **The entry's new evidence region cost the home two extra sequential round trips, on the arrival F-216 had just spent a packet making fast** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 1 (`cc9ac4e42`), found by auditing this lane's own surfaces against the rule it had published an hour earlier.** `readHomeAnswers` gates the home's first paint and awaited its reads one after another: the last look, arriving, the graded count, the releases, their deployments, and then the re-score count and the closed loop added that afternoon. **Eight sequential hops**, two of them mine, at roughly 275ms warm per hop on this deployment (F-212's own measurement). Only two of the reads depend on `lastLookedAt`, so the look stays its own hop and the other five leave together: **three hops now**, the look, the group, and the deployments read that needs the release rows' ids. **Extracted to a plain `readAnswers(supabase, userId, workspaceId)`** and `readHome` calls it with the request's own client, which is F-212's own pattern and was already applied to `readApprovalsQueue` **two lines from the defect**, with a comment saying why; this one was still a nested `createServerFn` re-running the auth middleware on every arrival. The extraction is also what made the shape testable, because the round-counting wire drives a plain function and cannot reach inside a server function, so `the-home-answers-are-three-hops.test.ts` now holds it the way the queue and the strip are held. **Two more the stricter typing and the guard turned up:** the `decisions` embed is TYPED as an array and RETURNS an object, and the title was being read straight off it (both shapes handled now, because the cost of being wrong is a silently missing subject line); and the default-workspace RPC's answer was taken with `?? null`, which accepts any shape it returns, so an empty array became the workspace id, every read filtered on it and they all answered **zero** in a file whose own header says null means "we could not find out" and must never become zero. The rule: **shipping the answer to a complaint out of the budget of the surface the complaint is about is not a fix; count the hops you add to a critical path in the same change that adds them.** | The home's arrival: the three answers and the evidence region. |
| **F-238** | **`held` and `failed` differed by hue alone on the run road, so a deuteranope could not tell a condition the loop will clear from an outcome that already happened** | **CLOSED 2026-09-09 by Lane 1 (`294f7b469`), the last open ruling in the design contract.** It had been left open deliberately rather than closed on one unverified finding, and it asked whoever took it to **measure first** and say which of three answers it was: a second non-hue channel, a ring style, or `failed` being `done` with a fail verdict. **It is the first.** Measured: every other channel is spoken for. The glyph is the STATION's and not the state's, which is law 4 working as designed (identity is shape). The word beside a current node is `labelOf(here)`, the station's own name, so `held` and `failed` drew the same word in different hues. The dashed ring is `waived`. The fill was the only one free, and it is the channel `stopped` had taken a finding earlier for the same kind of reason. **The axis is one a person acts on: the road stopped here and will not continue on its own.** `stopped` is that with a person required; `failed` is that with the answer already in; `held` is a condition the loop may still clear, so it keeps the lift and stays visibly lighter, which is the true reading. **`done` keeps the lift too, deliberately**, because it is six nodes out of seven on a finished run and filling those would make the road heavy, which is the founder's own "looks AI-made" complaint: **the fill marks the node that STOPPED the road, never every node that is over.** Measured after: `--mrd-fail-chip` L 0.32 against lift's 0.215 on the dark ground, about **1.48:1 in greyscale where the two rings alone were 1.11:1**, and better than the 1.24:1 the `stopped` fill was accepted at. No new token; the chip pair had existed for both hues all along. Verified on the served build at real size on a stopped node (`2ed8933a`, track `6cc7a010`): next to six unfilled neighbours it is clearly the node the road ended at, without being loud. | The run screen's road, the home's run rows, the journey map. |
| **F-239** | **The Meridian gallery drew none of the four states whose whole question is whether a person can tell them apart** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 1 (`80d16e484`).** The gallery's own exemption in `route-inventory.test.ts` says it exists "so a design is looked at before it ships, which is the failure this repo has already paid for twice". It drew three roads and not one of them carried `held`, `stopped`, `failed` or `waived`. **That is why F-238 was argued about in a document for a day: there was nowhere to see it.** One road now carries all four plus `unread`, with a note to read the row in greyscale, which is the test law 3 sets. **It is a DEV-ONLY page**, and I learned that by sending two lanes to it on production and watching it bounce to the home: its `beforeLoad` redirects unless `import.meta.env.DEV`, because it renders fabricated data and a customer must never meet that (P-10). The route inventory's exemption said only "reached by typing the URL", which reads as though production serves it; corrected in the same sweep. **Looking at this row means running the dev server and signing in there**, which is a real cost and worth saying out loud rather than implying the page is one click away. Same commit retires a noun the canon dropped that afternoon: the gallery drew "3 drawings" under Design. The rule: **a workbench that does not draw the states a ruling is about cannot settle the ruling, and the states most worth drawing are the ones that differ only by status.** | The Meridian gallery; any future ruling on status paint. |
| **F-240** | **An exemption in the route inventory said a surface was "reached by typing the URL" while its route had redirected everything but a dev server for eleven days, and nothing was checking** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 1 (`5575089e0`), from an incident, with the guard's shape proposed by Lane 3.** `/meridian`'s exemption gave its reason at length and ended "Reached by typing the URL". Its `beforeLoad` has thrown a redirect unless `import.meta.env.DEV` since P-10, because it renders fabricated data and a customer must never meet it. **I read the exemption, believed it, added a row to that gallery specifically so a ruling could be looked at, told two lanes to go and look, and then opened it on production and was bounced to the home.** Nothing went red and nothing could: **documentation is the one place a claim can rot with no failing test**, and an exemption is worse than most because it is the ONLY place anybody looks for how a surface is reached, so the whole point of writing one is that the reader stops checking. **The guard is deliberately narrow and must stay so.** Most of an exemption is prose and a guard on prose is the trap this very file was bitten by twice the same day. One claim is mechanical: a route whose `beforeLoad` redirects unless `import.meta.env.DEV` is unreachable in production by any means, so its exemption must say so, and both halves are greppable. Proved by restoring the old wording and watching it name the route and quote the sentence. The rule: **a claim about how a surface is reached must be checkable against the route, or it is a comment that outranks the code in every reader's head.** | The route inventory; every exempt route, public and authenticated. |
| **F-241** | **The most-mounted read in the product spent a whole round trip on four joins whose keys the database already held** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 3 (`e59d65380`).** `readStudioSessions` backs the station strip, which mounts on every authenticated page. Eleven hops on 09-08 became three; the third round existed only because four of its reads were keyed on what the second returned: the file count per changeset, the title of a spec found through the lineage, the cost events of a trace found on a checkpoint, and the slug of the agent an unrun proposal was routed to. Every one is a join, and **a join is the one thing the database can do without being told the answer first**. One PostgREST aggregate over the existing foreign key `studio_changes.changeset_id` and three functions in migration `20260909101000` moved all four into the round that already holds the key. Two hops. **The method is the finding: each function was run on production against the reads it replaces BEFORE the caller moved** (60 newest runs, the 80 oldest carrying no `trace_id`, every mission's lineage, every routing; zero disagreements, cost totals equal to four decimals), because once the caller is on the new read there is nothing left to compare it to. Two LEFT JOINs are deliberate and both have a real row behind them: six missions point at an agent that no longer exists, and a spec the caller cannot read keeps its lineage link with a null title. **An inner join is a claim that the nicety exists, and when it does not it deletes the fact instead** (Lane 1's phrasing). | The station strip, every authenticated page. |
| **F-242** | **A money figure was summed on every authenticated page and rendered by nobody** | **FIXED 2026-09-09 by Lane 3 (`3343b18e3`), measured by Lane 1 while auditing the swallowed errors around it.** `StudioSessionListItem.cost_usd` had no reader: every `.cost_usd` in `src` is a different object or a different column. So the read that mounts on every page coalesced each run's trace from two places and summed `ai_events` over it, and dropped the answer. The field is deleted and `run_trace_costs`, written five hours earlier to make that same sum cheaper, is dropped with it in `20260909101100` rather than left in the schema for the next reader to work out. **An unread number cannot be made honest, only deleted** -- the flag that would have said "this could not be counted" was the wrong fix for a number nobody counts. **And verifying a read against production proves it is correct, never that anyone wanted it**: the verification pass an hour earlier made the read feel load-bearing. Two swallowed errors in the same handler were answered differently on the evidence: the missions read now throws, because an empty list is drawn as seven stations with no work in them and the strip's own "count unavailable" state is only reachable by throwing; the gate count travels as `null` rather than a throw, because `run-state.ts` already catches `waiting_approval`, `blocked` and `proposed` from the status itself, so the exposure is one run whose status still says running while an approval sits pending, and an unread count now resolves toward the look. | The station strip; `run-state.ts`. |
